GCW — Pay-per-view

Warpath 2008, 30 July 2008

30 July 2008
Content noticeA period document, served unedited. It carries the humor, language, and content typical of its era and community — some of it crude, dated, or offensive, including material that wouldn't be written today. See Curation.
Warpath 2008 SkyDome — Toronto, Ontario ---- Introduction The GCW logo flashes across the screen, then everything goes dark. The Warpath logo follows, exploding onto the screen amid a wall of flames! Images of Chris Bagwell, Andy Murray, Shaman, Bryan Mayhem, LeStatt Knight, Chris Storm, Jordan White, Jay Terror and Vivica J. Valentine flash across the screen, before finally giving way to a massive eruption of fireworks in the arena! The Air Canada Center illuminates and the MegaTron flashes to life, sparking a huge roar from the capacity crowd. The cameras begin panning across the frenzied fans as the voice of James Bryan begins the show. Bryan: With over twenty-five thousand screaming fans on hand, Global Championship Wrestling is live on Toronto, Ontario, Canada for WARPATH, one of the most important nights in GCW history! Tonight, GCW brings to you a stacked lineup of some of the biggest matches of the year, culminating in one of the most historic GCW Title matches in history! Hello, everyone, thank you for joining us tonight on Pay-Per-View! I'm James Bryan here at ringside once again with the incomparable David Yale, and Dave, this is the night we've all been waiting for! Yale: Thank you, JB, it's damn good to be back on Pay-Per-View, and this one has all the makings of one of the biggest shows in the history of this promotion. There's a lot at stake tonight, JB, and Warpath could end up changing the landscape of this company for a long time. Bryan: We've got thirteen exciting matches tonight, and every title in GCW will be on the line! We've got a best-of-three deathmatch series between Bryan Mayhem and Shaman, we've got a huge closed cage match between Andy Murray and Chris Bagwell in one of the most personal matches in GCW history, and we've got a huge match between LeStatt Knight, GCW's first ever Grand Slam Champion, against Chris Storm! Yale: It's a huge lineup, JB, but you've got to be looking to the main event. Vivica J. Valentine's incredible title reign is finally going to come to an end tonight, at the hands of the man of destiny, Jay Terror. Bryan: That's been the buzz all week, Dave, that Terror's time has come. But Valentine is hoping to fulfill her own destiny and get that decisive win she's been searching for... And the sleeper in the match, if it's even fair to call him that, is Jordan White, who has his own destiny to reach tonight! Yale: What a night this is going to be, JB. Bryan: And I'm being told we're not wasting any time! We're going to get this show kicked off with the first of three deathmatches between Bryan Mayhem and Shaman! Yale: Wow! Here we go! ---- Bryan Mayhem vs. Shaman I The crowd is shown the back parking lot. Saw horses, hard hats, a big hole in the ground, and some construction machinery is seen. The crowd erupts. Warpath has begun, and thus begins round one of the very first Russian Roulette match in GCW history. Yale: Well it’s time for the bloodshed to begin. Wow what a way to start off the card. What are you doing? James Bryan is taking a pill with some water. Bryan: Taking anti-nausea medication, because I’ve got a funny feeling in my stomach that this is not going to be pretty. Camera goes back to the site and standing on either end of the hole is Mayhem and Shaman. The crowd explodes, and their thirst for blood will be quenched in a matter of moments. The staredown continues. Both men have a history together, and both men absolutely hate each other. Shaman is mouthing something, and Mayhem’s stare can cut the tension like a knife. Yale: Wow, this is going to be intense, but now its just a matter of time before these two explode. Shaman begins to almost foam at the mouth, and Mayhem bares his fangs. It’s time! Both men almost on cue jump across the hole and smash in mid-air landing in the bottom of the hole with a thud. Shaman is up quicker and begins to lay a fury of rights and lefts on the head of Mayhem. Ignoring the pain, Mayhem explodes to his feet and blocks a right than a left and begins to lay a barrage of fists against the skull of his former partner. Yale: Wow look at the speed and intensity these two are bringing! Bryan: Remember there is one and possibly two more matches of this caliber left tonight between these two. Mayhem’s fists seem to have an impact because it is slowing down Shaman. Mayhem goes to get out of the hole when his pants are grabbed by Shaman. He begins to pull Mayhem back in when Mayhem reaches and grabs a construction helmet, turns himself and wails on Shaman with a deafening crack. The helmet almost disintegrates in Mayhem’s hand. Shaman loses his grip and falls back. Mayhem climbs from the hole and stands on the edge looking down at a now bloody Shaman. Yale: Wow that helmet just busted Shaman wide open. You wanted blood everyone? Well it’s starting to flow early. Bryan: Let’s be honest, did you really thing these two weren’t going to make each other bleed? Shaman begins to come too and that’s when Mayhem backs up, takes a run and baseball slides right into the face of Shaman knocking him down. Mayhem gets back up, backs up again and waits again. Yale: He almost seems to be lining up Shaman. Shaman gets back up again and this time moves out of the way as Mayhem goes for the slide again, this time missing and sliding into the hole with Shaman. Shaman wastes no times and begins to pound the body of Mayhem with kicks and knees. He’s aiming for the right leg and knee. Mayhem begins to scream in pain. Bryan: That’s the same knee Knight tore up in Belmont, and Valentine re-injured in the Buried Alive Match. Shaman knows Mayhem’s weaknesses. Shaman climbs out of the hole and begins to survey the area. Yale: Oh no, you know what he’s looking for…a weapon! He spots the backhoe, wipes the blood from his eye, and shows the world that sick hideous grin he’s known for. The crowd gasps when they see what he see’s. He turns around and is met with a blast. Yale: Wow! Mayhem somehow got out of the hole and introduced Shaman’s skull to Mr. Drywall! Shaman’s legs buckle but he does not go down. The cut over his eye is deeper and bleeding more heavy than it was. Mayhem aims and lays a devastating right to the cut. A spurt of blood erupts from the cut, but Shaman won’t go down! Bryan: Tables have turned. Mayhem never goes down, and now he’s having a hell of a time getting this monster down himself. Mayhem backs up runs and tries to lay a clothesline, but Shaman ducks it. Mayhem turns around and is met with a boot to the chin that drops him into a pile of paint cans. Shaman turns back around, walks towards a porto-potty and attempts to lift it. Bryan:Can he do it? With an awesome display of power Shaman gets it off the ground but that ends when Mayhem comes flying from off the screen and kicks out Shaman’s legs. The potty falls on both men smashing them under. Yale: Let’s hope it’s empty. Bryan: Nope. Look’ Mayhem and Shaman both kick the potty off them and are both soaked. The crowd gasps and lets out a EWWW Yale: Wow that’s a first. Bryan: Yeah those pills aren’t working. Both men look at themselves than at each other. They are soaked with God knows what, but that doesn’t stop them. They both attack like rabid wolves and begin to throw rights and lefts wildly. Yale: Now it’s a street fight and both men don’t care what they are covered in. Shaman gains the upper edge and lands a knee to the gut of Mayhem. Mayhem keels over and his face is met with that exact same knee. Mayhem’s head rips back as blood spittles from his mouth. Shaman looks behind him, picks up a 50 pound bag of concrete and drops it over the back of Mayhem’s head. The bag tears and dust emerges from everywhere. Mayhem is covered in a grey dust. He’s down to one knee when another bag is busted over his head, creating another cloud of dust. Yale: Wow look at that! Mayhem is a..a…well a ghost? Bryan: How long have you been with this company? You just figured that out? Shaman grabs one more bag and one final time busts the bag over Mayhem’s head. Another camera comes in from a far away angle because the dust has overtaken the entire screen. Hal Jenkins and Shaman both are coughing up lungs. Mayhem is laying on the ground, covered in dry concrete. The concrete is a dark gray over his eyes and his neck where it’s caked to the blood and sweat he has let out so far. Shaman walks away coughing and gagging. He’s ripping at his eyes. Some of the dust got into his eyes. Jenkins is doing the same. It looks like the aftermath of New York on September 11. Plumes of dust everywhere. Both cameras can’t see a thing. They are running around to gather whatever video they can get, but nothing can be seen. Commotion is heard, and all of a sudden the dust is blown away from the screen and all that is seen is the pile of cry concrete that Mayhem was lying in, but no Mayhem. Bryan: Abbra Caddabra and than there was none. Yale: Mayhem has vanished and Shaman just realizes this. He must have cleared out his eyes. For those at home we had a commercial fan blow the dust away. Shaman looks a little nervous. He knows what Mayhem can do when he does this disappearing stuff. Shaman is walk around almost circling to avoid any attack from behind. He is on his toes expecting the worst, anticipating his enemy to appear from nowhere. A loud thud is heard and Shaman quickly jumps and watches as the arm from the concrete swings over the hole and begins to rotate and dumps wet clumpy concrete into the hole. Yale: Shaman may have youth, and at this point in Mayhem’s career the strength, but let’s be honest when it comes to head games there is none better than Bryan Mayhem. Bryan: Yes but the poise of Shaman is also amazing. Most men would have made an accident in their pants by now. A clink and a sound of metal on metal is heard behind him. Shaman jumps again and looks behind him, but there’s nothing but cameramen and the ambulance. THE AMBULANCE! Shaman races towards the ambulance. The EMT workers quickly jump from Shaman’s path. He enters the back than hears a beeping sound and than notices the forklift is backing up. Yale: He has Shaman running around like a fool right now. Bryan: I don’t understand though. Shaman is not like every other wrestler. He doesn’t spook easily. This is just making him more mad, and that’s not a good idea. Shaman races towards the forklift and notices a pipe wedged into the foot panel causing it to move. Shaman rips it from the floor. The forklift stops, and Shaman holds a death grip onto the pipe. Yale: I have a feeling he is not going to let that pipe go. MAYHEM! ENOUGH WITH THE GAMES! FIGHT LIKE A MAN! Shaman screams into the air. A squeal is heard and Shaman turns towards his back again and speeding after him in reverse is the ambulance! Shaman in the last second jumps from the way of the runaway safety vehicle. The ambulance screeches to a halt. The door blasts open and a grey smoke covered Mayhem climbs out. In fear Shaman dropped the pipe, but doesn’t realize he dropped it until Mayhem is right on him. Mayhem lands a headbutt to the face of Shaman, and a knee to the gut. He wraps his hands around the throat of Shaman, lifts the 300lb monster in the air and throw his on top of the ambulance. The glass from the windshield shatters and slivers like a spiderweb. Bryan: I think it’s going to get ugly. Mayhem climbs on top of Shaman, straddles his chest, and begins to smash Shaman’s head into the glass. YOU TRIED TO KILL ME YOU SONOFABITCH. NOW IT’S YOUR TURN! Mayhem inches from Shaman’s face screaming. The head bashing continues. The glass is crunching with the impact. Shaman’s eyes close and he lays there motionless. Yale: Oh God No! Mayhem stands up on the hood of the ambulance, his beaten and bloodied friend lies there motionless, a pool of blood erupting leaking from his head. Mayhem calls over Hal Jenkins. He puts a foot on Shaman’s chest. 1 2 AHHHHHHHHHHHH!! Shaman grabs a shard of broken glass and slams it into the ankle of Mayhem causing the scream. Mayhem falls from the ambulance and lands hard on the ground holding his ankle. He pulls the glass out and tries to get up wobbling, limping, unable to put any sort of pressure on it. Yale: How the hell does Shaman do it? He sits up slowly, teeth gritting. He grabs the back of his head and pulls his hand away. Its covered in blood. He smiles slightly than spits. He rolls off the hood and watches as Mayhem tries to stand up. Shaman starts to laugh. He now has the edge. Your enemy can’t fight if he can’t walk. Bryan: This is going to make this interesting. Yale: What’s amazing is this is the first match! We still have an amazing card ahead of us tonight. Wow it’s living up to the hype so far. Mayhem is screaming in pain, holding his ankle, while Shaman, beaten and bloodied stalks him. Walks around him like a circling vulture. Yale: Now the tables have turned a bit don’t you think. Mayhem makes it to his feet, but they are immediately kicked out from under him by Shaman. Shaman is laughing at this. The blood from his head is seeping down to his chest and back, but it’s almost like he doesn’t feel it. He’s driven to make Mayhem pay. Mayhem makes another attempt to stand up, and once again he’s kicked back down to the ground. Mayhem just lies there, almost looks like he’s giving up. Shaman squats down and begins to mumble something to Mayhem. Mayhem just looks at him, while Shaman continues to speak. Yale: If I could be a fly on the wall right now? Bryan: What wall? We are outside, what possible wall are you talking about? Shaman stands up, Mayhem cocks his head and looks right up at Shaman. Shaman is about to walk away but quickly turns around and lands a boot right between Mayhem’s eyes. His eyes roll up and he falls backwards. Shaman spits on him than walks off. Bryan: Where the hell is he going? Shaman comes back carrying two saw horses one under each arm. The crowd senses blood and begins to go into a frenzy. He sets them up about 4 feet apart. He pushes on them to make sure they are sturdy. He looks back over at Mayhem who is stirring a little but nothing to be worried about. Yale: This does not look good for Mayhem at all. Shaman walks back towards Mayhem who is now bleeding between the eyes. He pulls him and drags him towards the saw horses. Mayhem is in a daze. Shaman smacks him in the cheeks to almost wake him up. Bryan: Well round one is in the bags. I’m going to get a hotdog, you want anything? Yale: Sit down! Shaman sets him up. Yale: DESERT SPIKE! Mayhem on the way up struggles and struggles. He’s blocking it. Shaman is trying to lift up the monster but to no avail. He puts Mayhem down. Mayhem stands up, knee’s Shaman in the gut and lifts him up. Yale: Oblivion Bomb is coming! He lifts the 300lb monster over his head and drops him down on the sawhorses. The sawhorses splinter from the impact. Shaman lies motionless in the carnage. The crowd is ravenous and explodes! HOLY SHIT! HOLY SHIT! HOLY SHIT! Bryan: Yes what they are saying! Mayhem rolls over and lays on Shaman. Jenkins gets down for the count. 1 2 3!!! The crowd erupts! The winner of round one, and leading 1-0 of this best of three match. BRYAN MAYHEM!!!’ Bryan: Oh my God what a way to start! Mayhem takes a lead into the scaffold match later on tonight. All he has to do is win that and he wins the first ever Russian Roulette match in GCW History! Yale: That was an incredible kickoff to this Pay-Per-View, JB! The camera shows Mayhem lying on top of Shaman. Both men are breathing heavy, but the man on top right now is Mayhem. Bryan: Mayhem has taken down his former partner Shaman, but the stakes will only get higher as this series continues! Yale: I don't think there will be any winners in the war between these two monsters, JB. ---- A Proclamation of Victory Ready for battle? Possibly. As she walks alone down the hall, Daria's mind is planning strategy for her match, though she knows that she goes in as the underdog. Twice beaten by Zachariah Taylor, and outsized by Dan Black, it will take every ounce of speed and ability she has to pull it out and leave with the bauble, the GCW TV Title belt that she craves. So intent is she on her thoughts that Sally Ford speaks her name twice before she finally realizes that somebody is talking to her. Sally: Daria! Let's get your thoughts on tonight's TV Title match. Daria: I'd really rather not. Sally: You missed Worldwide 94 last week, and the week before were hit pretty hard by the man currently carrying the belt around, Dan Black. Do you have any words for him? Her first impulse is to slap that fake smile off of the announcer's face. Her second impulse is more of the same, yet the Congo native's eyes roll back in her head as she represses those urges. Barely. Daria: Dan Black played me. He used my emotions, flirted with me, even went so far as to take me out on a date. Do I have any words? Oh, you know I do. Unfortunately they aren't words I can say on television. She pauses and looks right into the camera. Daria: So, Danny boy, I'm just going to say this. Enjoy being flat on your back staring up at the arena lights, and enjoy watching me walk away with the title. Hell, enjoy whatever sort of pep talk Bagwell gives you before the bout. Because you will only be hurting after this is over. Sally: What about Taylor? Daria: I respect him. But I'm going to end his run tonight. That is all I have to say. She shoulders past Sally, thrusting her back into the wall as she turns a corner, once more lost in thought. ---- Garbage Bag Johnny vs. CJ Newfield vs. Timmy Lester Bryan: This next one should be interesting. We've got three GCW newcomers -- Garbage Bag Johnny, CJ Newfield and Timmy Lester -- all facing off to try and showcase their talents in GCW. Yale: So we've got a veritable athlete in Newfield, and then it just keeps heading downhill from there. Timmy Lester is a "B Movie" actor and Garbage Bag Johnny -- well, I don't know what he's supposed to be. Bryan: Needless to say, he's undefeated here in GCW after two matches. Newfield has split two, and Lester is one for three so far. All three have shown loads of potential so far, though. Yale: Camal Litigation is on my Netflix queue. Andrews: The following contest is scheduled for one fall, and it is a triple threat match. Introducing first, from Chicago, IL; Garbage Bag Johnny! Smoke begins to waft through the entranceway as the rubber-bandy tone of sweet, sweet slap-pop bass kicks off "Garbage Bag Johnny Will Win Zero 2 Hero." There once was a man called Garbage Bag who sat on his couch all day With a bong that he bought with his welfare check to smoke his life away The THC would fill his lungs and make him feel nice and numb Johnny's making himself dumb, oh well he's just a bum Garbage Bag Johnny (Garbage Bag Johnny) Garbage Bag Johnny (Garbage Bag Johnny) Garbage Bag Johnny (Garbage Bag Johnny) Garbage Bag Johnny (Garbage Bag Johnny) Garbage Bag Johnny appears in front of the capacity crowd as his own vocal line repeats his own name. The riled up Canadian crowd gets to their feet and begins to cheer. Yale: I still don't get it, JB. That's a much nicer reaction than I usually see homeless people get. Bryan: Garbage Bag Johnny is a unique character, but he's starting to show that he isn't a fluke. I think he knows a lot more about being in that ring than you'd ever know from talking to the guy. He's just a stain on society; a stranger to sobriety He won't amount to anything. He smells like old cat piss He swears like a fucking sailor, and he scares the little children And he used to have potential to be so much more than this Garbage Bag Johnny (Garbage Bag Johnny) Garbage Bag Johnny (Garbage Bag Johnny) Garbage Bag Johnny (Garbage Bag Johnny) Garbage Bag Johnny (Garbage Bag Johnny) (Unintelligible gibberish about a fish sandwich) Yale: What's this? He's asking Joey Andrews for his microphone! Bryan: I guess he has something to say. Joey Andrews nervously gives up the microphone after some persuasion on GBJ's part. GBJ: Cut my music! As commanded, the music stops. Garbage Bag Johnny starts chuckling. GBJ: You know, I've always wanted to say that. Now turn the music back on. That song is a jam. Garbage Bag Johnny waits, but this time, his directions go unheeded. Yale: If Garbage Bag wants to hear his music again, he's going to have to win the match. Bryan: That might be all the motivation he needs, though. It's an interesting song; I'll say that much. Yale: It sounds like whales dying . . . or breeding. I can't really tell. GBJ: No deal? Fine, then. Now there was a reason I came out here, and that reason is to talk about the reason I came to GCW in the first place. Yale: It looks like GBJ is coherent as ever tonight. GBJ: I was passing by a pawnshop one evening, and I took a look through the window at a pyramid of televisions. WorldWide 92 was playing on one of them, and there was a gentleman I thought I recognized. He looked like yours truly, just as grizzly but a little older. Something about that grizzle, though. It was remarkably unmistakable. They called him Cary Turkington, but I knew it was myself from the future . . . or maybe I was myself from the past in the future. Either way, I needed answers. What was I doing at a place like this? How did I get here? Why did I change my name to something so utterly stupid? Was I hiding from the government? Bryan: It looks like Joey's really regretting his generosity with the microphone. Yale: I'm going to kill him. GBJ: When I arrived at GCW, a man named Octavian took care of me. He seemed like he was trying to help me out at first, but still disoriented from the space-time conundrum I had seemingly developed, I was unable to realize at the time that he was controlling me with some sort of mind control device. Yale: I think he's talking about a megaphone. Johnny has a tendency to follow amplified voices. Bryan: It wouldn't surprise me if those accusations were true, though. Yale: Why would Octavian need to manipulate a loser like Johnny? And why would you believe a reefer fiend over one of the most important figures in our company? GBJ: The point is, I think Octavian might have something to do with the fact that I'm here in the future, or that I came back from the future to the present to warn myself about something that I, myself, have no idea I have to warn myself about. I'm swearing an oath that I won't sleep until I found out what it is and take down all the sons of bitches who are trying to stop me, and I have enough methamphetamine to make good on my word. At this point, Joey Andrews steps in and apprehends the microphone. Garbage Bag backs off and strokes his beard in a corner. Yale: Thank God. Bryan: Just pretend he didn't say that last part. The lights cut out in the arena, prompting the fans in attendance respond with a chorus of screams. "Fortune Faded" by the Red Hot Chili Peppers explodes from the PA system as the arena is illuminated with green and gold lights. Special gold spotlights highlight the entranceway as a figure makes its way out into the spotlight. Head down, the figure strikes a statuesque pose, arms straight out from his sides in a cross figure, before lifting his chin to reveal himself as the "Paragon" CJ Newfield. Andrews: Now making his way to the ring, from Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania; CJ Newfield! Yale: Did you know that in Pittsburgh, they put the fries directly on their cheeseburgers? Bryan: You learn something new every day. Carter James Newfield has one of the most impressive finishers I've seen. It's called the Parabomb, and it's a modified fisherman's suplex into a Death Valley Driver. Yale: I guess you do learn something new every day. Newfield slowly saunters his way towards the ring, peering around the arena, taking in the moment of his first GCW PPV appearance. The fans give a mixed reaction, some of the audience remembering his win over fan favorite Clyde Fox at WorldWide 94. Once he reaches the ring, CJ slides inside and takes his place atop one of the ringposts, striking the same pose as he did to signal his arrival. Bryan: There isn't too much of a discrepancy between these competitors. Timmy Lester is the largest, but only by about thirty pounds, and Newfield is right there in the middle. Andrews: The final competitor in this triple threat match up, hailing from Hollywood, California by way of Utica, New York; Timmy Lester! ZZ Top's "La Grange" queues up, and white strobe lights start to flicker. Lester enters the arena smoking a cigarette and wearing sunglasses and a black robe. He slaps hands with the Toronto faithful on his way to the ring as the crowd cheers him on. Lester puts his cigarette out on the back of his left hand, slides into the ring and extends his forearms with his biceps against his body. He flings the rope off and waits for his foe. Bryan: Referee Hal Jenkins signals for the bell to begin the contest. DING! DING! DING! The three competitors eye each other up from opposite corners until Newfield finally lunges forward to fake a move. The fake catches the charged Lester, who jumps into Newfield for a collar and elbow tie-up. Garbage Bag Johnny, meanwhile, shows a completely opposite response, sliding out of the ring and looking for the rest of the cigarette that Lester hadn't finished smoking. Bryan: I don't know what kind of strategy this is. Yale: I think you're confusing strategy with idiocy, JB. Lester uses his slight size advantage to power Newfield against the ropes and send him catapulting with an Irish whip. Lester waits crouched as Newfield returns and drops him with a back body drop. On his way up, he sees Garbage Bag Johnny outside the ring smoking his cigarette. Lester takes a few steps to push off before diving feet first between the second and third ropes to floor Garbage Bag Johnny with a missile dropkick. Bryan: Brilliant maneuver by Timmy Lester. This one's starting off with some sauce! Yale: I think Garbage Bag Johnny is still smoking that cigarette. Indeed he is, sitting against the guardrail, Johnny has managed to clinch the cigarette between his lips. Timmy Lester walks over to Johnny and pulls him up by the hair. Johnny totters around on his feet and takes an unshielded hook right to the face. Timmy Lester can't believe that GBJ is still standing with the cigarette in his mouth. He double takes and decides to throw another punch. Johnny takes this one in stride as well. Timmy's jaw drops, and Garbage Bag quickly grabs the cigarette and flicks it at Timmy Lester. Lester avoids the cigarette but steps right into a sloppy enziguri kick by GBJ. Bryan: Timmy Lester is getting back up to his feet in the ring, and Hal Jenkins is just going to let it go. There's no point to having a count out in a triple threat match. The more time you spend outside, the likelier you are to lose. Yale: It's not like Jenkins would've counted anyway. I hear he's afraid to count past six. Bryan: Why would a referee be afraid to count past six? Yale: Because seven, eight, nine! As both men get up on the outside from the weak enziguri, CJ Newfield takes off and leaps over the top rope, knocking both Garbage Bag Johnny and Lester to the mat with a cross-body splash. Newfield has the agility to twist after impact and somehow land on his feet. Bryan: What an athletic maneuver by CJ Newfield. He's like a Tetris piece. Yale: That's what you can do when you don't smoke before your match. Newfield pulls Garbage Bag up by the hair and rolls him under the bottom rope. CJ climbs onto the apron. CJ slingshots himself over the top rope, landing a leg drop across Garbage Bag Johnny's sternum. Newfield drops down for the cover. ONE! TWO! Garbage Bag kicks out with little effort, but CJ remains in control. He pulls Garbage Bag Johnny up to his feet and whips him off of the ropes. Newfield backs up, but he doesn't notice that Lester is on the apron, he sends a standing sidekick over the top rope to the back of Newfield's head and Newfield stumbles forward past Johnny who keeps running towards Lester. Lester grabs hold of the top rope and swings his legs through the second and top rope while momentum pulls that top rope down. Lester plants two feet into Garbage Bag Johnny's stomach and pulls backwards, sending Garbage Bag Johnny flipping high into the air with a painful landing outside the ring in store. Yale: That was tremendous, JB! Inventive monkey flip by Timmy Lester, and Garbage Bag Johnny is seeing stars -- even if they are only B-Movie stars. Bryan: I have a feeling that it's going to be Lester and Newfield for a while as Johnny recovers. Lester wastes no time getting back into the ring, and Newfield stumbles into a belly to belly throw. Lester follows it up with a knee drop before inverting his body and crashing down with an elbow. Timmy Lester for the pin! ONE! TWO! CJ Newfield kicks out with authority, giving himself a chance to get back to his feet. Bryan: These two still have far too much life in them. I don't know how Johnny over there is faring, though. Yale: I think I could guess. CJ and Timmy start exchanging blows in the ring, and both men appear to be feeling it from the other. They trade three rounds back and forth before Newfield ducks a hard shot from Timmy Lester, kicks him in the gut and immediately drops Lester with a spiking DDT. Newfield covers Lester. ONE! TWO! THR - Lester kicks out just in the nick of time. Bryan: I thought that might have been it. Newfield caught him out of nowhere with that spiking DDT. Yale: That move definitely looks like a match ender. CJ Newfield pulls Timmy Lester back up to his feet and backs him into the turnbuckles. CJ whips Lester to the opposite set of turnbuckles. Newfield charges at Lester, but Lester manages to get a foot up. Newfield dives under the foot and slides out of the ring. Before Lester can even get his foot down, Newfield grabs him by the shins and pulls, tripping Lester flat on his face. Newfield wraps Timmy Lester's legs around the ringpost and readies himself to pull the B-Movie star balls first into the unforgiving steel until Lester's jewels are saved by the grace of Garbage Bag Johnny's recovery. GBJ dropkicks Newfield in the back of his head, slamming CJ's face against the ringpost. Yale: I think CJ might need some smelling salts after that one. Bryan: It looks like that cut him open a little bit. As blood starts to trickle from Newfield's forehead, Garbage Bag Johnny hops up to the apron, climbs agilely to the top rope and jumps down, smashing a double stomp onto the back of Lester's head, his face pushed against the apron. GBJ then retreats a few steps before baseball slide dropkicking Lester right in the crown. Lester's body jerks back and the same kick that saved him from this fate earlier just drove his Porno Mummies into the ringpost. Bryan: Garbage Bag Johnny is on absolute fire getting back into this match. AXEL became a star and another star left us. ---- Open Season Barry Goldstein stands with mic poised center screen in front of a grey and red backdrop bearing the Warpath 2008 logo. Goldstein: Welcome backstage, where Jordan White has requested an interview with yours truly. He's also requested that I focus on him and his involvement with the sport, so I'll be avoiding going too far into his competitors. The camera pulls back, revealing Jordan a few feet away, his beard and hair both trimmed. He wears his brand, his JXW, in loose khakis and a brown polo shirt. His thumbs tap a coda against his legs; he nods on introduction. Goldstein: I've got to say, before we start this, that I've never been a fan of you or your apathetic take on this sport. White: Why do you think I wanted you here? Seems kinda pointless if I just let somebody that liked me interview me on a night like this. Some preachin' to the choir bullshit. Goldstein, eyebrows momentarily rising: Fair enough. I'd like to begin with something that's been troubling me since you arrived: how exactly can you work in a business like this with the disdain you clearly have for it? White: If you're good at somethin', shouldn't you do it? I mean, okay. The pay's good, the benefits are damned incredible. Are you tellin' me I should quit now that I'm back in title contention? I've said it before—I lucked out gettin' my job with GCW, but that doesn't mean I'm not gonna try to get everythin' I can out of it before I'm done. Goldstein: You honestly can't expect to win the world title tonight. Both of your opponents have consistently shown a willingness to defy limitation, to drag themselves to the breaking point to get what they want. What could you have in response to that? White, stifling a laugh: A tougher breakin' point. I mean, okay. V's got all the heart in the world, but her record against me's already one loss and one loss by DQ. Terror gets himself pissed to the point of distraction, and doesn't even have Boss Caldera around to help him out tonight. What I'm sayin' is, in spite of how much they might want that belt, I'm alright in the ring. Your colleagues been stressin' that a lot lately. Sometimes, sometimes bein' passionate just means you go out a martyr. You know? Goldstein: Still, Valentine and Terror are both immensely talented wrestlers. Vivica's reign as champion's easily one of the greatest in GCW's time. Terror holds victories over yourself, LeStatt Knight, and the late Rich Rollins. You don't dismiss these people that simply. White: Hah. Yeah, yeah, I sorta do. It ain't like Terror's gonna beat me in a fair one-on-one. The boy's never tried—there's a reason. He knows, deep down, that even when he was hangin' around me an' shit, he was a little brother. He got Caldera on his side so he wouldn't have to try. Terror's a little crazy, from what I know, but he's not stupid. Jordan smirks, running hand back through his hair as Goldstein briefly swallows words back down before redirecting himself. Goldstein: I don't think that fear was a primary motivator for that. The briefcase of money and the chance at holding GCW's highest accolade did well enough in their own rights. White: Maybe. I'm just lettin' you know how I shrug 'em off. You brought it up, man. What I'm gettin' at is that, their heart, their passion? Those things can hurt somebody just as soon as they can help 'em. Frankly, when I think about it, I don't think they're as talented as me, and in the real world, where we're not heroes and villains, where we're just jackasses in spandex? A lot of fights go to the better fighter, not the one that cares more or worked hardest. If I went to their level, if I put my soul full bore into this game they play? I wouldn't have an edge. That apathy, that anarchic act you hate? That's my advantage. In a career where mental stability isn't exactly required, stayin' level headed's a good way for job advancement. Goldstein: So, you think not giving it your all is fair to the fans that pay your salary? White: Look. I like to think the people that watch this aren't watchin' to see who cares the most. The sport's called wrestling, and that's what I do, that's what I give them. How's that not fair? Goldstein: Treating all of this like a game certainly isn't fair. Not to the people that came before you, not for the ones that will come after you. In making yourself famous, all you're doing is tarnishing the entire legacy this is built on. White: My predecessors, they did what they did and I can't take away from that. Far as the kids watchin' now that'll be wrestlers someday? They shouldn't, man. This is a dangerous career with so few positives that I've got to pretend the paycheck's worth it—would you want your kid gettin' involved with somethin' that's as likely to break his neck in a bingo parlor as actually earn him a dollar? I don't want to think I'd convince somebody to get involved with this, anyway. He shifts his balance side to side, breathes calm and seems to focus entirely on Barry. Goldstein: That's exactly the problem with you. The best wrestlers are the ones that inspire those around them, the ones that advance the game with their drive and their determination. Vivica Valentine has pushed more boundaries than you ever will, all because of how far she'll take herself. If you dedicated yourself, I might agree with the people that see something in you. It's just that all of your talent is wasted on someone that doesn't give one damn about the sport, past or future. White: Not everybody shows affection the same way, Barry. V loves the sport by wantin' to keep the title for herself and not let it fall into the hands she decides are dirty, Terror loves it by smashin' people's heads in on little rages. I know where I stand; I give the fans a third option. You want to know why I don't play the game, why this is my first and probably only serious interview on a GCW show? Goldstein: Go ahead. White: Because every second they spend establishin' that they're good or bad is another second the people that do pay all of our salaries don't get to spend watchin' us do our fuckin' job. I don't get paid to play out stupid dramas on television, I get paid for the same reason as the true pioneers of this sport did: because they could beat people. I'm proof that you can do your job without turnin' Worldwide into an hour of wrestling and two hours of arguin'; I only play those stupid games when I have to to save my own ass. That I do have apologists, as you called 'em, means that I'm not the only person more interested in the ring than what the backstage looks like. They don't waste half the time before a fight showin' boxers bitch at each other, do they? Goldstein leans his head to the side, digging his free hand into the pocket on his sports coat as he wavers the microphone lightly. He looks to Jordan; Jordan shrugs. Goldstein, clearing his throat: Well. Isn't it that drama that distinguishes us from boxing, though? We're beyond being base violence—what you call arguing, what you're calling a waste of time, is an escape for a lot of people. Were Vivica to retain tonight, doesn't it mean more that she's doing it against a man that stands for nothing and a man that stands for everything she's against? White: Maybe to her, man, but the entire thing about keepin' wrestling separate from the other sports just hurts us in the end. Every athlete goes through shit like that, all we do by emphasizin' it is is come across like the sport itself doesn't matter. You say I hurt wrestling? Nah, nah. The people that turn it from a competition into a soap opera hurt it. And maybe we just disagree on that, but I never took you as the kinda guy that preferred the pageantry to the heart of the thing. Now, don't get me wrong, wrestling's never been my first choice for a job, but I'm here, and I can do it well. Sometimes, sometimes you've gotta change your goals to suit your environment. That's all I've done. Goldstein: What exactly would those goals be, then? White: Hah. Just because I've explained myself a couple times don't mean I wear my heart on my sleeve. Think about it like a wish: they lose their power if you tell people. Honestly, I think it's about time I get back to the locker room. I'd hate to lose focus on the night. Goldstein: Thanks for your time, Jordan. White: Shit. Got nothin' else to do 'til my music hits. Thanks for your time, Barry. He waves and exits stage right; the camera recenters on Goldstein with something of an uncharacteristic smile cracking the corners of his mouth before we return to ringside. Bryan: Jordan White's certainly been paying attention to his detractors since his rise to the top. Just because he starts saying the right thing to get a few more people on his side doesn't mean he actually cares. Somewhere in the arena, illuminated by a couple of spotlights. The old-school promo setup usually only wheeled out when PPV time comes around, but tonight there is no Sally Ford, Kevin Jacobs or Barry Goldstein to introduce the proceedings. There is only a man. Dressed in black and gold ring attire, he paces back and forth, the adrenaline pumping furiously through his body making it impossible for him to stand still for any length of time. For a moment he glances up and pulls the hood of his robe backwards. His face looks tried, worn down, as if he’s not slept in a couple of nights, but the fire in his eyes belies any notions of fatigue. Murray: Been in this game, what… ten, eleven years now… He looks like Andy Murray, dresses like Andy Murray and moves like Andy Murray. His voice, while hoarse and gravelly, still has a distinct North-Eastern Scottish tinge to it that can only belong to Andy Murray. By all accounts, the man before us resembles The Scottish King of Cool in just about every way imaginable. Murray: Fought across the world; been here, there and every-fuckin’-where. Stunning highs and terrible lows, peaks and troughs. Did it. Done it. Doing it again. But something’s different. There is no boundless enthusiasm or mile-wide grin, and no smart-assed wisecracks escape the Scottish King’s lips. His self-professed ‘overwhelming aura of coolness’ has been all but demolished, replaced by an overpowering sense of malevolence and dread. And then there’s the look in his eyes; nought but pure, unbridled focus and unrelenting determination. Murray: I’ve been here before, Chris. Andy glances upwards again. A small nasal strip runs horizontally over his nose, the one remaining sign of last week’s Bagwell-orchestrated Movement sneak attack. The thick layer of stubble peppering his chin only adds to the wickedness oozing from the man’s pores. Murray: Seven years ago, almost to the day, I last stepped inside an enclosed steel chamber. Fought long and hard for thirty minutes, ‘til a man by the name of James Rande decided it’d be a good idea to throw ol’ Andy from the top of said structure. You know what happened next, Chris, I don’t think I need to document the havoc that the ensuing back injuries wrecked upon my career. He smiles. It’s sharp, short and almost maniacal, and it doesn’t lost for longer than a couple of seconds. Murray: So you see, Chris, this is a big night for me in a number of ways. Not only do I get to wipe that fucking smirk off your face once and for all… but tonight I come face-to-face with the man-made structure that has robbed me of so much of my professional career. Tonight is about more than you, Chris Bagwell. Tonight’s about putting old demons the rest, about putting the final nail in the coffin of a spate of misfortune that has hung over my head like a vulture for far, far too long now. Still running from pure adrenaline, Andy paces back and forth again, but quickly abandons this, realising he still has an address to deliver. Thirty of the top wrestlers in the world in the ring at the same time, all battling for the most coveted opportunity in professional wrestling! The winner will receive a guaranteed World Title shot in the main event of the premier event in wrestling, NC-17. Yale: And this event has routinely made huge stars, JB. Dangerous Games has always been a launching pad for greatness, and everyone who has ever won this match has eventually gone on to win the World Title. Twizzy, Michael Stevens, Lia Ambrosi, Vivica J. Valentine, all have etched their names in the history books, and this year someone else will have the chance to add their name to that historic list. Bryan: I think you left one out there, Dave. Yale: Yeah, but we don't talk about him. Bryan: True enough... This year's Dangerous Games will be coming to you from swinging London, England, a great city that has always been a great host to GCW. It's going to be a massive event, fans, and this year's Dangerous Games match could be the most competitive in GCW history. Yale: There's so much incredible talent in GCW right now, JB, it's really anyone's ballgame this year. This match is going to make a new star, and it could be anyone. Of course, I'm just saying that because it's what the script says. In reality, there's only one clear choice, despite the result we just saw. Chris Bagwell is my favorite to win this year's battle royal! Bryan: Bagwell could certainly be in the mix, but he's got twenty-nine other wrestlers all vying for the same coveted prize. Last year Vivica J. Valentine won that event, and tonight she's here as GCW World Champion with a chance to stake her claim as the all-time best! It all begins at Dangerous Games, and it comes your way in September, only on Pay-Per-View! ---- LeStatt Knight vs. Chris Storm Yale: Well JB, as I understand it it’s now time for an execution. Bryan: Not sure about that but it IS time for Chris Storm to go one on one with LeStatt Knight, a match that is now a couple months in the making. Yale: It all started with Knight approaching Tessa Windsor, Storm’s fiancée, with an offer to help mentor her, assist in her living up to her potential. Which she gladly accepted. Bryan: Knight went on to end Banned & Exiled’s incredible tag team title run, becoming GCW’s first Grand Slam Champion and more importantly, putting Markus Stone in a wheelchair. Yale: Then in a genius move, Knight made STORM his new tag team partner … great mind games by the crafty veteran I’d say. Bryan: Maybe Dave, but now it’s all come to a head. This is personal for Storm, he hates LeStatt Knight and that could be the deciding factor. Yale: Sorry JB, Knight is too good and too experienced. I said it before, this is an execution! So...you want to be a Rock Superstar? The words cause the crowd to go crazy, all of them standing and cheering their lungs out. The chants hit all at once: B&E! B&E! B&E! Then Chris Storm walks from behind the curtains and into plain sight, this causes the crowd to grow even louder, showing their support for one of their favorites. Storm is geared up as he usually is, Punisher hooded sweatshirt and matching black leather pants. Tonight he still has his calm and casual walk towards the ring but there’s something different. Storm doesn’t wave to the fans on his way down tonight, doesn’t throw his arms into the air for their support. His eyes are focused, HE is focused. Like usual he DOES however grab a microphone before heading into the ring. He walks to the center and the crowd hushes, allowing their hero to speak. Storm: There are no jokes tonight. I’ve been waiting for awhile for this very match, this very moment. So LeStatt Knight, get your ass down here and let’s FUCKING DO THIS! The crowd erupts as Storm throws the microphone down! Yale: JB can you hear me!? Bryan: WHAT!? Of course Knight is only so happy to oblige. With that, the lights abruptly go out, plunging the arena into darkness. The crowd, intimidated, drops to an awed hush. After a pause, the ominous, theatrical voices of "O’Fortuna" begin to sing overhead. The music drops out, and a slow, sinister chant begins overhead. Slowly but surely, the stage begins to ignite. As the music rises in intensity, the flames grow higher and higher, rising from beneath the stage, reaching towards the rafters. As the slow, quiet, menacing music continues, a dark figure appears on stage. Suddenly the music begins booming with terrifying music as LeStatt Knight snaps his head up, peering down to the ring from behind a wall of flames. The flames abruptly drop before Knight as he steps forward, before shooting back to the sky as he passes! Knight stops short of the ring, slowly his arms begin to lift into the air but in a quick motion he thrusts them upwards, and the intimidated crowd manages only a weak round of boos before retreating once more. Knight crosses to the steel steps and slowly rises to the ring, where he steps through the ropes. Hidden behind his cascading black hair, Knight stops in the center of the ring, where he finally throws his head into the air, raising one arm over his head. Four tall jets of flame explode from the turnbuckles, illuminating the arena once more. The boos are deafening. LeStatt Knight and Chris Storm’s eyes never leave each other as Knight slides back into an opposite corner. The tension is now sky high. Bryan: Listen to this crowd Dave! They have been waiting for Chris Storm to get his hands on LeStatt Knight for awhile now and tonight he finally gets the chance. Yale: But is that a good thing JB? Storm was impressive with Stone in the tag team ranks but what have we seen from him otherwise? While on the other hand everyone knows just how vicious Knight gets when he is in the spotlight. He’s damn near unbeatable. Bryan: Maybe that’s true Dave but Storm has something going for him that Knight doesn’t … this is personal. Storm has grown to completely and utterly despise LeStatt, his hatred should fuel him! DING-DING! At the sound of the bell LeStatt and Storm walk to each other, meeting in the center of the ring, toe to toe. The cameras cannot catch what’s being said from the two superstars but it’s almost certainly not very friendly. It only takes a few moments before the conversation is heated and only a few ticks longer than that for Knight to forcefully shove Storm away. BAM! Storm fires right back with a hard right hand which blasts Knight on the cheek. LeStatt staggers back and Storm is immediately in his face again firing off quick, powerful right-left-right combination that drives LK to the ropes. Chris ensures Knight doesn’t have time to think by bodying him up and quickly bringing his knee up into LeStatt’s mid section and quickly shifts his body to the side and swings his arm around, grinding his elbow across Knight’s jaw. Bryan: Storm’s on fire here quickly Dave! Yale: Blasphemy! He’s assaulting a Hall of Famer! Storm stays on the offensive; grabbing Knight by the head and hair he rips him away from the ropes, twists his own body and places it against Knight’s and forces Knight over into a variation of a hip throw. LK flips over Storm and crashes to the mat where he lets out a quick yelp of pain as Storm never releases his grip on Knight’s shoulder length hair. Chris reaches down with his free hand now and grabs another handful of hair and angrily pulls Knight back up to his feet. As soon as LK gets to his feet he twists and throws a stiff right hand that smacks Storm square in the face. The blow stuns storm for a moment but he catches himself quickly and in time to grab Knight by the hair again as he was running the opposite direction to bounce off the ropes and yank him down to the mat. LeStatt hits back first on the mat but immediately rolls over onto his stomach, one arm going underneath his body to push him up, the other clutching the top of his head, probably checking for bleeding. Storm doesn’t allow Knight to get any higher than his right knee before he’s back on the attack, helping Knight to his feet. LeStatt is on the wrong end of three forearm shots that push him back into the ropes and make him open to Storm getting in an Irish whip. Knight reverses! Storm bounces off the ropes and meets Knight in the middle! FLATTENED! Storm ends up hitting the mat from a hard shoulder block. Understanding this is a bad situation; Storm rolls away from LK and gets to his feet closely. Storm locks the hold in perfectly, often grinding the bones in his own forearm into LeStatt’s nose area. It only takes moments of this uncomfortable state for Knight to forcefully shove Storm off of him and into the ring ropes. Storm flies back towards Knight, who goes for a clothesline but Storm ducks under the blow, stops and turns to face Knight. LeStatt turns as well and tries to go after Storm but Chris is a step ahead and drops to a knee and sends Knight flying over his body with a Fireman’s carry and transforms that into a sleeper hold with Knight in the seated position. Bryan: Incredible combinations by Storm, he’s not letting his rage blind him, he’s very focused. Yale: He’s trying to wear Knight down, good call I’d say. That does indeed seem to be part of Storm’s plan, take away Knight’ explosiveness which is one of his greatest strengths. It also may be close to impossible without killing him. Never the less, Storm is in command here a sleeper hold locked in deep. Chris is a strong guy but Knight is far superior in this category so it’s not quite so difficult for him to maneuver his way back to his feet and with a couple of inches on Storm, he easily gets Chris in a position where he doesn’t have the leverage needed to fully take advantage of the move. Knight’s plan is to twist and then hit Chris with a side suplex, breaking the sleeper hold and causing some damage in the process BUT Storm feels this coming. Knight twists but Storm let’s go of the sleeper a moment soon and basically rolls off of Knight’s body and steps away. LeStatt turns in time to get planted with a Side Shuffle Kick to the chest. Of course the blatant choke comes next followed by referee, Tim Vale’s mandatory count for Knight to release the hold. LeStatt, obviously, knows that Vale would never end the match on something so petty so he holds onto the choke for a few seconds longer than he is supposed to. LeStatt only loosens his grip for a second, to break the count, before going right back for the choke. Knight runs through the same process one more time before releasing Storm for good and taking a step back. Chris latches onto the top rope and leans over, coughing over and over again. Valentine vs. Jay Terror vs. Jordan White The camera returns to ringside, where the buzz is beginning to build. The shots slowly pans over the massive crowd as the house lights slowly dim. How is this happening?! Valentine slinks to the corner away from her fallen opponents and lowers her head onto the turnbuckle, cradling the GCW Title belt against her chest. Finally she inhales deeply and steps onto the ropes, raising the belt into the air in thanks to the roaring crowd. Bryan: What a moment! What a champion! Valentine has just won the biggest match of her life! Nobody thought she could do it, but damnit, she pulled it off! Yale: I still don’t believe what I’m seeing, JB. This is all wrong. Something terrible happened here tonight! Valentine cheated fate! Bryan: Terror definitely seemed to have this match won, but Valentine snuck in at the last moment and pulled off that incredible Destiny Driver! And it was Destiny that cost Terror what he felt was his! Valentine finally drops out of the ring and, overcome with emotion, begins slapping hands with the stunned fans at ringside. She slowly makes her way away from the ring, clutching her title belt hard. Bryan: And there goes one of the greatest wrestlers in this company’s history! What a gutty victory! There were a number of times in that match where it looked like she was finished, like she couldn’t possibly continue. She absorbed an inhuman amount of punishment, but somehow she just kept coming until she pulled it off! Yale: Ok, ok, fine, Valentine won this match, but this is NOT the end, JB! It can’t be! The journey does not end here! Bryan: Whatever the future holds, this night belongs to Vivica J. Valentine! Jay Terror and Jordan White will have to wait for their times to come, because Valentine has bested them here tonight! As Valentine nears the top of the ramp, Terror slowly begins to pick himself up. He glares up the ramp and sees Valentine walking away with the title held high, and suddenly he is overcome with rage. He drops out of the ring and marches towards the announce table, making a b-line for Bryan. Bryan: Oh, what do you want now? Come on, Jay, take it easy- Terror delivers a hard shove, knocking Bryan off his seat from behind the smashed announce table. Terror snaps up his chair and turns back to the ring, where White is weakly gathering himself. Yale: Whoa! Terror’s livid! Bryan: That son of a… Just as White reaches his feet, Terror winds up with the chair and brings it straight down over his head. The awful metal crack ripples through the arena as the crowd explodes in boos. Valentine’s music fades as the champion has made her exit, and Terror begins screaming incoherently at the crowd. Bryan: Terror has snapped! He has absolutely lost it here! Yale: This was supposed to be his night, JB! This night was about Jay Terror! Bryan: And he’s taking his rage out on Jordan White! This man was your friend, damnit! White left his heart out here in the ring tonight and now he’s being assaulted! Terror raises the chair and slams it down into White’s side, and follows with a sadistic barrage of shots. Briggs tries to pry the chair away, but Terror shoves him down hard and delivers another crushing shot. Bryan: Come on, someone stop this! Terror is out of his mind! He’s destroying Jordan White! Yale: Someone’s got to pay, JB! If Jordan White weren’t in this match, Terror would have the title! White just got in the way! He shouldn’t have even been here! He’s had his chances, this was Terror’s moment! Glaring down at White, the blood dripping off Terror’s face and onto White’s chest, Terror finally opens the chair and wraps it around White’s ankle. The crowd moans, knowing what’s coming. Terror quickly hops onto the second turnbuckle and leaps, bringing a massive stomp down on the chair! Bryan: OH MY GOD! Terror just crushed White’s ankle! Yale: Serves him right! White should not have been in this match! Bryan: Quit making excuses! Terror lost and there’s nothing he can do about it! He’s just taking all his rage out on Jordan White! Satisfied with White writhing on the mat, clutching his ankle, Terror thrusts his arms into the air and earns a monster rush of boos. He finally staggers towards the ropes and drops out of the ring, lurking up the ramp with a glassy look on his now-blank face. Bryan: God, what the hell is going on in the mind of Jay Terror?! It’s like he’s completely lost his mind! Yale: It was all riding on this, JB, and Valentine stole the show! Terror had his entire life, his entire career, riding on this match! Bryan: Terror… God, that was vile! White’s ankle might be broken after that assault! These were two men that used to have each others’ backs, and Terror has completely annihilated White! All in the name of his so-called destiny! Terror reaches the stage and slowly looks back over his shoulder as medics rush to the aid of Jordan White. Terror eyes him with his eerie blank stare before staggering out of view, and the GCW logo appears on the screen. Bryan: Fans, we’re out of time from Toronto! Jay Terror has attacked Jordan White, but it’s Vivica J. Valentine that’s riding high tonight! She’s defied all the odds once again and retained her GCW Championship! The Valentine Era continues in GCW! For David Yale, I’m James Bryan, thank you for joining us on Pay-Per-View… GOOD NIGHT! The show fades as Jordan White is helped to his feet, a distraught and angry look on his face as his eyes gaze after the former number one contender….
